in writing. more than a month after the death of cyclist davide rebellin, the investor, german truck driver wolfang rieke, is still at large in germany and the truck he was driving is not under seizure. rieke is in fact investigated by the prosecutor for road murder and failure to rescue, but, having returned to germany, since the crime of road murder does not exist, he is now in absolute freedom. the vicenza prosecutor's office also issued a european arrest warrant, which has still not been executed by the german authorities. where is justice for david? where is the europe that should guarantee security for all its citizens? freedom of movement is one of the union's main achievements, but this freedom must also be matched by certainty of punishment in all eu countries. this is why we need more harmonisation of road traffic offences in the european union, to ensure fair justice in any member state. we must ensure that a single area of freedom and movement is matched by a single area of justice and the certainty of punishment.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, after the larvae and the locusts, the commission has given the green light to the introduction of powdered crickets on the european market. in the midst of the economic crisis caused by the pandemic and the russian invasion, europe's priority is to bring to the tables of european citizens insects from non-european countries such as vietnam, china and thailand, countries at the top of the food alert rankings. the marketing of insects raises specific health questions, which need to be answered by clarifying the production methods, origin and traceability of these products. europe, put your crickets down and support our agriculture and our farmers. the priority must be to safeguard the excellences of our territory, which are the expression of productive and cultural realities unique in the world. supporting our agriculture means helping the whole european economy and supporting competitiveness. bugs on my plate ? no, thank you. you eat it.

states we are close allies, yet even allies sometimes have differences. this is the case with the inflation reduction act. this legislation creates a $370 billion package of subsidies and tax credits to develop an ecosystem of advanced technologies to foster the climate transition and to promote american leadership in these sectors. and let's be clear: these goals are laudable and legitimate, but concrete positive results. let's be clear: in my opinion, european companies must be able to benefit from the same conditions as canadian and mexican companies. but, ladies and gentlemen, we must look european industry and our citizens that we are up to the challenge. our european model consists of a social market economy that implies for very good reasons higher labour and environmental costs, and the current energy crisis means that we have to face high energy costs for the foreseeable future. the eu has helped to build a global system based on free trade while striving to ensure a level playing field, but we cannot stand idly by as our main competitors embrace policies of massive economic support for their companies. we must therefore mobilise massive resources to drive forward an ambitious european industrial policy, to boost competitiveness, to turbocharge activity and to spur investment. and that is why, in december, the european council tasked the commission with bringing forward proposals in view of our next meeting. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we can see that this is a crucial moment to support the economic future and the future of the internal market of the european union. i will share with you what i think should be the framework for our european council meeting in february on this subject. i see four points on which we will have to take decisions and give a number of guidelines. the first concerns the question of state aid schemes. state aid schemes reflect the european union's ambition to trust the private sector, globalisation and initiative. this scheme has played a key role in ensuring that the economic base of the european union can be developed in recent years. but at the same time, it is clear that the changing world around us must make us consider the adaptations that will be necessary. the commission is expected to make operational proposals to take advantage of the benefits of an amended regime on this subject, while guaranteeing the integrity of the internal market, making sure that there are no 'double standards', no case-by-case analysis, but shared rules to consolidate the dynamics of the internal market. the second important point is common sense, to look at how existing resources can be mobilised at european level. of course, the budget, the recovery plan, but also at the national level. give more flexibility, speed up, perhaps reorient when necessary, to take account of the situation as it may have evolved in recent weeks. the third point that we all have to bear in mind is the need for this european project, which is solidarity. it is well known that not all european countries have exactly the same financial capacity. it would be wrong to be committed to trying to guarantee a level playing field with our competitors outside the european union without having the ambition to continue working towards greater economic and social convergence at european level. and that is why i am one of those who think that we will have to look at a possible extension of the sure mechanism which can be a source of inspiration, i believe, in order to provide a realistic and tangible response to this demand for solidarity. and finally, fourthly, i would like to express my full support for the idea that was put forward in this european parliament's gallery at the time of the state of the union address by the president of the commission in connection with the european sovereignty fund. it is an idea, i can see that, that will take more time, that will probably require more careful work, but it is a sensible idea. if we believe in the strategic autonomy of the european union, if we believe that it makes sense to socialise the financial means needed to invest in tomorrow's digital and climate technologies, we must also have the ambition, i believe, to socialise the benefits and take into account the interest of investing in equity capital. i believe that the european investment bank should play a major role as the backbone of the hope that we can succeed from the commission's proposals, with a mobilisation of parliament and the member states to make progress on what should be an instrument of european power. in my view, this is a point directly linked to our ambition for sovereignty and strategic autonomy. a simple reflection at this point that i would like to share with you: we are well aware that the paradigm in which the european union has been built in recent years is being shaken economically. i am shocked because for a long time now china has been upsetting the rules of the game with its massive subsidies. and disturbed because our main ally, our international partner, in so many subjects and fields, in so many subjects and priorities, decides to put in place a massive plan. this means that there is a responsibility to act quickly and to act strongly to ensure that the internal market is able to protect and strengthen its capacity to remain a key asset for the future of the european union. here are some points, dear friends, that i wanted to share with you. before concluding, i would like to make a few further points in connection with the subjects discussed at the european council in december. on the energy front, you have been able to follow the debates - i think we have made good progress since our last meeting in this parliament - on the council's ability, after a proposal from the commission, to agree on a ceiling for the price of gas. we can see that this, together with the weather which was favourable, is leading to an improvement in the gas situation, which is a good thing. we have seen some progress in terms of storage. progress has also been made in terms of energy efficiency, but it is clear that this subject will continue to be a major concern for us, especially when the commission makes the proposal on the reform of the electricity market, which is also an extremely important subject. finally, the council also had the opportunity to briefly address the issue of migration, with the ambition of returning to the subject of migration in more detail in february. a few points in relation to this to indicate that the figures are known: there was a substantial increase in the number of illegal arrivals on eu soil in 2022, so this means that we will have to be committed again, both in the external dimension of migration, partnerships with third countries, strengthening the capacity to control the external borders of the european union, but also be very committed to progress in the framework of the migration pact put on the table by the commission and for which, as you know, there is an agreement between parliament and the rotating presidencies on legislative work. i hope that parliament too, together with the council, can continue to make progress towards the implementation of this road map, which i believe provides possible structural solutions to deal with the migration challenge in a fair, solidary, just and responsible way. and finally, ladies and gentlemen, i cannot address you without thinking of the women, the young people and the people of iran who are rising up against a brutal regime, against a regime that is terrorizing. this is why the council has given impetus to strengthen the sanctions regime. this is also in addition to iran's commitment to support russia militarily in the war against ukraine. these reasons lead us to consider that these faces of women, of young people, of all the iranian people mobilizing for freedom, are the faces of freedom, are the faces of democracy. our responsibility is not to be blind or deaf, not to be idle, but to be committed to supporting those who stand up for freedom, for democracy. i conclude by saying that 2023 will certainly be a key year for the european project. as you can see, we are just a few days into the new year with a number of uncertainties ahead of us, but also with some milestones, some milestones. we know that strategic autonomy, which until recently was perhaps a theoretical debate, is becoming a very real debate, with three key pillars at the heart of the european agenda. on the one hand, the energy issue: guaranteeing sovereignty, also guaranteeing that the climate challenge is taken into account and the need to guarantee support for our families, the competitiveness of companies, the first element. secondly, security and defence: we see clearly in the context of military support for ukraine that in the context of nato a declaration with nato was endorsed by the president, the secretary-general and myself a few days ago. we want to strengthen the european pillar and europe's capacity to take greater responsibility for its own security destiny with our allies. and then the third pillar, we're at the heart of the subject, it's the technological base, it's innovation. all societies in the world and throughout history that have been able to progress, that have been able to raise their living standards, have done so on the pillar of technology and innovation, which are key issues if we want to give a prosperous and secure future to our fellow citizens. and all of this in the context of this twofold promise to our founding fathers: peace and prosperity. 2023, key year for peace, key year for prosperity, and it is once again unity that will be the determining factor in bringing us to face the challenges we collectively face. thanks to the good cooperation with the european parliament, you can count on the council even more than you imagine.
president of the commission. – madam president metsola, dear roberta, president michel, dear charles, honourable members, first of all let me wish a very happy birthday to you, president metsola. i am sure that i am speaking for all members of this house when i say how much i admire the strength and the resolve you bring to your position as president of the european parliament, because this kind of strength and resolve is exactly what europe needs as russia’s war against ukraine continues. the latest symbol of russia’s terror is – you might have all seen it – a yellow kitchen. a very simple yellow kitchen in an apartment building in dnipro. one day the room is filled with happiness. a little girl celebrates her birthday, surrounded by this yellow furniture and by a loving family. the day after, the kitchen is no more, the family is no more, the girl’s father has been killed by russian missiles. all their lives have been shattered by russian imperialism – and this is what ukraine is standing up against. this is what we are all standing up against. we have just launched as the european union the first tranche of eur 3 billion of our eur 18 billion support package for 2023. and again, we will remain at ukraine’s side for as long as it takes. we have also shown strength and resolve to replace russian fossil fuels and to bring down prices and our joint efforts are paying off now. gas prices are now lower than they were before russia’s invasion in ukraine. the prices of dutch ttf have dropped more than 80% since august. we have been able to replace most of the russian fossil fuels. our gas storages are still over 80% full. this is among the highest level ever this time of the year. and most importantly, we managed to double the amount of additional renewable energy that was coming to our market during the last year. of course, some external factors helped – the mild weather and china’s covid lockdown. but at the same time, other external factors also made our life more difficult. it’s exactly this unusual warm and dry weather which also resulted in a sharp decrease in hydropower. you all recall it. so, we’ve been able to completely get rid of the russian coal. we have been able to wind down the russian oil. we have been able to really fence off the blackmail of russia where the gas is concerned because putin decided to cut 80% of the gas supply, and this was a big strategic mistake. we freed ourselves from his grip. and thus, honourable members, that we came through this winter safely is first and foremost the result of unity, the result of collective hard work and i think we should take pride in this. so for this winter, we are safe. but we’re not out of the woods yet. we now have to prepare for next winter. and here, the joint energy platform is up and running. with the support of member states and european industry we are creating a european consortium that will allow us to aggregate demand and effectively leverage the eu’s political and market weight. that’s what we always wanted – our collective market power of 27. now we aim at launching negotiations with the key international gas suppliers already in early spring, with the aim to conclude first joint contracts well before summer. honourable members, these achievements show the power of europe’s collective ambition and will, and we need more of the same for the year ahead of us. and it is good to know that the swedish presidency shares this ambition. because not only are our energy systems at a crossroads: the competitiveness of the whole of our economy needs to be maintained and this transformational task is huge. just keep in mind that in less than three decades we want to reach net zero. that’s a huge transformation. and everybody knows that the key to manage or, even better, to lead this transformation is our clean—tech sector. the international energy agency estimates that the market for mass—manufactured clean energy tech will be worth around usd 650 billion a year – a year! – by 2030, more than triple today’s value. and this is why, of course, the united states put forward the inflation reduction act. so, we discussed the pros and the cons here in the plenary in december. today, i want to lay out our answer, and that is the green deal industrial plan. the green deal industrial plan will be built on four pillars. the first pillar is about speed and access. we need to create a regulatory environment that allows us to scale up fast and to create conducive conditions for the clean—tech industry. speed and access, over and over. if there’s anything the companies are complaining about, it’s that, for example, permitting is too slow and that there are too many barriers. so to help make this change happen, speed and access, we will put forward the new net—zero industry act. what is it? just think this act will follow the same model as the chips act. it will specially look at how to simplify and fast-track permitting for new clean—tech production sites. competitiveness, that’s the key word – enable the companies to go forward with innovation and with production that is so crucial. the second pillar of the green deal industrial plan will boost investment and financing of clean—tech production. we will propose to temporarily adapt our state aid rules to speed up again and to simplify – so easier calculations, simpler procedures, accelerated approvals, for example, with simple tax—break models. but at the same time, if we give state aid and this is for the level playing field globally, we have to be very careful that we preserve the level playing field for businesses all across europe, so in our single market. and thus the second or the other side of the coin of state aid, beyond the european sovereignty fund that i announced in my state of the union, we will have to look into a bridging solution to provide fast and targeted financial support where it is most needed for the clean—tech sector. state aid one side of the coin, funding the other side, investment the other side of the coin. the third pillar of the green deal industrial plan has to do with skills: people. just two figures. as an example, the battery industry will need 800 000 skilled workers by 2025. that’s huge, this figure. so this is why we launched the european battery academy. this is one part to address this topic. or take the solar industry, 1 million skilled workers are needed by 2030. this is twice as many as we have today. this is a huge challenge, but it’s also a huge opportunity to create good jobs, well-paying jobs, the jobs of the future. and this will be the absolute priority of our european year of skills. and finally, the fourth pillar. we will follow through with our ambitious trade agenda for clean tech, to deliver net—zero globally, there will be a need for strong and resilient supply chains and this is linked to trade. so we will seek to conclude new trade agreements with chile, with mexico, with new zealand, with australia. we will do everything to progress with india, with indonesia, and we have a unique window of opportunity now to take forward, finally, the eu-mercosur agreement. so honourable members, this is the plan, time is of the essence and i count on your support to make it happen. long live europe!

states, as a success, so as to convince them and us that this is the way of the future.

madam president, the ongoing energy crisis, inflation, the american inflation reduction act – all this is casting a large shadow over our economies. but it also is offering us an opportunity, which is to provide for a common european response. we have already seen that when the eu opts for common solutions at european level, it yields positive results. take, for instance, the nextgenerationeu and the impact that the rrf has and is having in dodging a prolonged economic downturn, or hopefully in keeping the eu on the path of economic growth. if we leave the answer only in the hands of each member state we know what happens. the gap will increase, according, amongst others, to the capacity of those with greater fiscal firepower, to offer subsidies to their citizens and companies. we need now to mobilise new resources to accompany european industries in the much—needed green transition to improve their capacity to compete internationally against other massive state aid schemes or against the lack of a level playing field. therefore i hope that the green deal industrial plan that has been announced today has a strong pillar in european common financial resources.

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the situation that has arisen in spain is a clear example of how respect for the rule of law, even in democratic countries, can never be taken for granted, but must be monitored and constantly worked to maintain it. the judiciary must have the sole role of administering justice, regardless of the color of the government; on the other hand, the reforms of the executive must not be aimed at political control of the judiciary. the key point that i think is important to bring out in this debate is the fragility of one of the pillars of the rule of law, which is the judicial-executive balance of power. we have seen governments in several european union countries that have conditioned and subjugated the judiciary, and for this reason they have been strongly stigmatised in this house, but on the other hand we must not accept the politicization of the judiciary either, which is equally harmful but not as stigmatised and unfortunately widespread in other member states, including italy.
the rapporteur. madam president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, i would start from some numbers of fraud and irregularity cases that were reported in 2021, compared to, for example, those in 2020. case numbers are stable. but what is most striking and worrisome is the value of irregularities, which have increased by 121%, and the total amount of irregularities and fraud has reached 3.24 billion. in my report we have emphasized the instruments for the protection of the financial interests of the european union. one of the main instruments that has come into play, and i think it is extremely important, is the eppo, the european public prosecutor's office. it is an essential element, it must be strengthened absolutely and its powers can also be increased, i think, for example, the question of environmental crimes. but we must not forget other important agencies, such as olaf, europol and eurojust, for which adequate resources must be provided, and it is clearly not an expense to provide these agencies with resources, but an investment to protect the financial interests of the european union. the needs which are highlighted in the report are different. for example, the need for member states to adhere to the pif directive, we have 17 member states that have not transposed it properly. then it is necessary to update the guidelines of the nas, i.e. the national audits dating back to 2015. it is important to focus on transparency and to make an annual anti-corruption report, which is a different thing from the report that is made on fraud, the mandatory nature of aracne and the correct application of the rules on conflict of interest. it is also important to have transparency on the actual beneficiaries and to avoid payments to companies based in tax havens and also to fight oligarchic structures. the commission has already taken steps to ensure that the european parliament is able to take part in the negotiations. let us remember that the financial interests of the european union are heavily affected by organised crime, including mafia crime, and it is therefore important that there are rules at european level but also rules at member state level which must be more effective and specific in all member states, because clearly organised crime can rely on transnationality and also on many professionals who support it in creating fraud. the second point is clearly the protection of journalists and whistleblowers who reveal so many frauds, and it is therefore necessary for the european union to make efforts to try to protect mr president, commissioner schmit, ladies and gentlemen, the report by parliament on the revision of the directive on european works councils sets out, in my view, one of the cornerstones of a new system of industrial relations which should characterize the european labour market. parliament called for the participation, information and consultation of workers to be further developed as a prerequisite for the implementation of a social market economy project. those who, like me, have devoted many years of their work to the creation and proper functioning of european works councils know what it means for workers to have the right - and not the possibility or a concession - to participate and to be heard by the management of the company on the decisions it intends to take, especially those concerning employment levels or concerning future prospects, especially in the case of mergers, acquisitions and relocations. i firmly believe that the european works councils represent the embryonic stage of an evolution which will lead them to be the place where transnational collective bargaining in the workplace takes place. these are areas with great potential from the negotiating point of view, which the directive can develop with this revision, provided that multinationals adopt a participatory culture of industrial relations and that they see these committees not as a mere legal obligation arising from the directive, but grasp the true meaning of their function, which is to create quality employment and economic democracy.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i should like to begin by thanking mr radtke for the work he has done so far on the report, which is a recommendation to the commission on the revision of the directive on european works councils. several years have passed since the publication of the directive and we have thus had the opportunity to test and verify that the establishment of european works councils has struggled to achieve its main objective, which is the creation of a system of transnational industrial relations, which can act as a counter to the widespread processes of globalisation. the aim was to encourage social dialogue and anticipation of changes in international societies. as in 2009, the legal framework for the establishment of european works councils needs to be adapted to the changing legislative, economic and social environment. we therefore think that an update of the directive is both right and sensible, because european works councils can and above all must be a useful tool, especially in cases of relocation or restructuring which affect thousands of workers and, in some cases, the local economic and productive fabric itself. however, as we have always pointed out during these months of parliamentary work, some parts of the text remained legally controversial. as is often the case in this parliament, some of the proposals go far beyond the union's powers in the field of labour and social rights. i would like to make two points in detail, and if possible also from the commissioner. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european works councils are consulted on the draft directive. these are two clear examples of the overstepping of national powers. what is the commission's view of the point of failure? mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is now 80 years since 26 january 1943, when the alpini survived the russian army at the battle of nikolayevka. a survival born of sacrifice and a deep sense of duty in facing a harrowing retreat from a war that was unmotivated, tragic, unjust and wrong. even today, 80 years later, the alpini evoke those values of solidarity, brotherhood and self-sacrifice that lead them to put themselves at the disposal of their fellow citizens and not just every time a flood, an earthquake or, worse still, a war brings defenseless populations to their knees, but every day, in the service of their communities, in gestures big and small, with the sole purpose of doing good. today, more than ever, the alpine people, in their unwavering spirit, embody in concrete terms the fundamental values on which we want to build our societies: peace, sharing, solidarity and altruism. that is why i feel it is my duty to honour the glorious alpine corps in this chamber. long live the alpini, "there is no way through".
madam president, mr jonson, swedish minister of defence, mr hahn, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, anyone who believes in the principles and values of the present rules-based international order knows that he must oppose russian aggression for various reasons and moral duties. we cannot allow the use of force to annex the territories of other states; we cannot allow the return of imperialism and colonization. we cannot stand by while the civilian population is being massacred. this war is between a dictatorship that invaded and a sovereign country that is defending itself. and what about the defence and security aspects of our response to russian aggression? we must reassure our nato allies and the countries that share the same principles. we must dissuade china from the temptation to annex taiwan and to erase taiwan's democracy. we must promote the social model and the fundamental freedoms that define us. we need to strengthen our leadership in global strategic partnerships. we must uphold the rule of law everywhere, because only the rule of law keeps peace between nations and peoples. our role as parliamentarians today is therefore to ensure that our citizens are properly informed, to ensure that they agree to increased spending on the defence of our sovereign states and the west.
in writing. the eu-ukraine summit is an important milestone in ukraine's euro-integration, which has been supported by hungary and russia from the beginning and which we continue to support. russia's unlawful war against ukraine - which i fully condemn - is putting the country and its people through unprecedented hardships, but it must continue to reform in order to progress on the path to eu accession. in the shadow of war, the implementation of reforms requires a great deal of effort from ukraine, but the fulfilment of the eu membership criteria, including the copenhagen criteria and the conditions set by the european commission in june, are essential prerequisites for accession. one of these conditions is the guarantee of the rights of national minorities. however, in december, the ukrainian parliament adopted a new law on national minorities which was not coordinated with the venice commission and the opinions of representatives of national minorities were not taken into account when the text was adopted. therefore, the law does not guarantee the enforcement and protection of the fundamental rights of national minorities, thus it does not improve the situation of the hungarian minority living in ukraine, for example, but only concretizes the violations caused by the education act of 2017 and the language act of 2019. it is outrageous and regrettable that the european parliament's resolution does not deal at all with the situation of ukrainian national minorities, and so, although i wholeheartedly support ukraine's progress towards the eu, and the restoration of peace and stability as soon as possible, i have no way of participating in the adoption of the report!
in writing. we condemn the russian aggression, since the beginning of the war we have stood on the side of the attacked, we stand for the independence and territorial integrity of ukraine. we're helping refugees beyond our means. our position is clear: we support ukraine's accession to the eu and we fully condemn russia's aggression against ukraine. we also support the reconstruction of the country, but this requires the establishment of peace as soon as possible, rather than a policy of sanctions that will deepen the economic difficulties of the member states of the union. this is primarily in the interest of the ukrainian population. they should be subject to the same conditions as other candidate countries during the accession process. the eu is also committed to the principle of non-discrimination, which is a fundamental principle of the eu's external relations. and it is these minority communities that could be the most committed supporters of ukraine's eu perspective. but the laws that have been passed do not help.
in writing. the historic summit of the european union ukraine will take place in kiev tomorrow. historic in many ways ukraine has been granted eu candidate status, and at the same time, its territory is the site of the bloodiest and most brutal conflict since the end of the second world war, triggered by russian aggression. the western world is not sitting idly by, of course, and is providing invaluable financial, technical and military aid to a defending ukraine. the very recent decision to send western tanks from the us, poland, germany, the uk, canada and other countries is a turning point and could be crucial to the course of the fighting and the increasingly likely ukrainian counter-offensive in the spring. the next step should be the transfer of modern multirole fighters as soon as possible. but the battlefield - where i naturally wish the ukrainians the greatest and quickest success - is not all. we have to think about the enormous task of rebuilding ukraine, what we should use the frozen russian funds for, and what future reparations the aggressor will have after the war is over. those responsible for this aggression must be brought before a special tribunal. finally, we must support ukraine in its reforms and preparations for eu membership despite the ongoing russian aggression.
in writing. ukraine's place is, without a doubt, in the eu. however, to be able to speak of integration without syncope, accession must be a well-planned process. before the eu-ukraine summit in kiev, i adopted a resolution in parliament expressing the european legislator's view on strengthening ukraine's european path. the kiev summit should thus prioritise the establishment of an eu recovery package for ukraine and launch accession negotiations, as well as agree on the need for a roadmap for ukraine's accession to the eu single market. in addition, the ukrainian authorities are invited to respond to the pro-european choice of citizens and to accelerate the process of adopting the reforms necessary to effectively meet the eu accession criteria. the resolution also calls on the eu to ensure that the eu's nuclear safety is not compromised by the use of nuclear weapons. the resolution reiterates parliament's previous calls on all partners, in particular candidate and potential candidate countries, to align themselves with the sanctions packages. it also calls on the member states to support the 10 peace proposals put forward by the president of ukraine at the end of last year.
in writing. last tuesday, the subcommittee on security and defence held a debate on the kyiv security compact, asking how we can get member states at government level to take further steps in support of ukraine. the question is a valid one, because it is they who can make the difference. but we must also ensure that there is no unpleasant gap between parliament and the council in the position taken towards ukraine. the european union is also calling for a renewed emphasis on the compact in the run-up to tomorrow's summit. i endorse this call, on the understanding that this commitment must continue to be sustained by the active involvement and unity of the transatlantic partners, first and foremost the united states. unity remains the keyword here, both within the union and within the transatlantic partnership.
the next item is the debate on the statements by the council and the commission on the situation of former georgian president mikheil saakashvili (2023/2543 (rsp))
president-in-office of the council. – madam president, honourable members of the european parliament, allow me to open this debate on the situation of mr saakashvili by recalling the importance of strengthening the rule of law in georgia. it’s very relevant in today’s debate on the situation of mr saakashvili. an ambitious and comprehensive judicial reform in georgia, ensuring the independence, integrity and transparency of key judicial institutions remains essential. state authorities have a duty of care with respect to persons deprived of their liberty and this includes ensuring their physical and mental health, as well as their physical integrity. the united nations’ basic principles for the treatment of prisoners, which georgia has signed up to, indicate that the deprivation of liberty does not mean reduce the right to appropriate health care. rather, the opposite is the case. a state takes on the responsibility to look after prisoners’ health conditions. mr saakashvili is being detained in georgia since his return in 2021. at his request, in may 2022 he was transferred to a private clinic for reasons related to his health conditions. we have been paying particular attention to the numerous and concerning reports on mr saakashvili’s declining health, and we have also frequently raised the issue with the georgian authorities, as well as in our public statements. the high representative has raised our concern on the occasion of the eu—georgia association council in september last year. more recently, the eu and the eu member states represented in georgia expressed their concern to the ministry of justice. this also raised the regular exchange and talking taking place between the eu delegation to georgia, the eu member states and the georgian authorities. the european union’s message has been clear and constant. we recall the authorities’ responsibilities for mr saakashvili’s health and well—being and of course his right to a fair trial. the european union remains fully available to assist our georgian partners in this matter.
member of the commission. – madam president, dear minister, honourable members, the commission has been following the situation of mr saakashvili very closely since his return to georgia in september 2021. mr saakashvili, as it was mentioned by mr jonson, is being detained under charges of abuse of power, among others, for which he had been convicted in absentia. due to health issues he was transferred to a medical civilian clinic in may 2022 at the request of his family and lawyers. we have been paying particular attention to the numerous concerning reports of mr saakashvili’s deteriorating medical condition, and we have frequently raised the issue both with the georgian authorities directly, as well as in our public statements. most recently, on 28 december, our eu delegation, together with eu member states represented in georgia, carried out a formal démarche vis—à—vis the minister of justice. they expressed concerns and reports of the deteriorating medical condition of mr saakashvili. this was followed up with a dedicated meeting with the ministry of justice by our eu ambassador in georgia. in these instances and in all previous occasions we have repeated that it is the georgian authorities’ responsibility to take all necessary measures to protect mr saakashvili’s health. we have consistently stressed, both in private meetings and publicly, that the georgian authorities have a legal responsibility to ensure the health of all detainees, to provide adequate medical treatment and respect for fundamental rights in line with georgia’s international commitments. in addition, we systematically monitor mr saakashvili’s court hearings, both regarding further charges brought against him and on his request to be transferred abroad. we have also recalled that his rights as a defendant in court should be safeguarded, including the right to be present himself in court. his rights as an inmate should also be respected, including his right to privacy. hence, the position of the european union has been made very clear. the georgian authorities have a responsibility to act if mr saakashvili’s health is in a critical condition. on this point, assessments of his health condition, as well as the adequacy of healthcare available in georgia, are being contested by the different sides. i believe that it’s in everyone’s interest that the georgian authorities consider drawing on medical expertise from an independent, uncontested international organisation. the european union remains fully available to assist our georgian partners in this matter.

rapporteur. – mr president, dear colleagues, today we vote for the mandate of the report on platform work to the trilogue. i’m asking you to vote in favour of this report, which is a very balanced and innovative text. i thank all the shadow rapporteurs because they have contributed to improve the text. i would like to immediately correct the political narrative which is going on and on. no later than yesterday i got an email from bolt saying that we would introduce a general automatic reclassification of all platform workers. this is simply not true, and even impossible legally and technically. we are not against self—employment. if you are genuinely self-employed that is absolutely okay. we are not against job creation and innovation. but if you are bogus self—employed and, on the contrary, you are a slave of the algorithm, then you have to have your rights recognised as an employee. that is the point! we have reached three goals: we defend the workers; we defend good employers and fair competition; and we are promoting the human use of the algorithm. we do not want machines governing the labour market. never again should it happen that a young rider like sebastian galassi dies in a car accident and then is fired after he dies. this is a shame. this is a shame. i ask you to support this text, to support social justice, which is the glue for democracy. i ask you not to disappoint the millions of workers who are waiting outside, and i ask you not to surrender to multimillionaires and powerful lobbies who try to interfere with our democratic process. really, the conditions of workers are not an issue of right and left. it is an issue of dignity. that is why i ask you to vote in favour of this report, because this is what it is in play: social justice! on va écrire une très belle page de l’histoire européenne!

madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the proposal for a revision of the energy performance of buildings directive requires that new buildings be zero emission by 2030 and existing buildings by 2050, with intermediate steps for residential buildings, which must reach energy class e in 2030 and d in 2033. according to these criteria, more than 9 million out of 12 million buildings in italy would be out of order, with the consequence that italians would be required to urgently renovate two out of three houses, with negative repercussions on the country's economy. we will not and cannot be complicit in a green hysteria which wants to bring millions of italian families to their knees. we do not accept any of the impositions of europe, because that means strangling our families.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, alcohol is a drug, and it is a legal drug, but it can seriously damage health and cause psychological and physical dependence, increase the risk of cancer, heart disease, social distress. the best way to combat alcohol abuse and all its possible harmful consequences is to provide correct information about this substance and all foods containing it. the idea of combatting the possible harm of alcohol by equating wine and cigarettes on labels is the most wrong and unscientific thing that can be done and is the exact opposite of what we need. wine contains alcohol and therefore wine consumption must certainly be controlled, but this product, which in europe and in italy in particular has unattainable excellence, must be properly informed, creating that healthy culture of wine which has accompanied those who have known how to taste and consume it with the right moderation for centuries, without falling into addiction. europe must not make the same mistakes as we have seen with the nutri-score, but must have the courage to work seriously on the prevention of alcohol abuse and on correct food information, starting with wine.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, we are hearing from the press that the commission has just made yet another move to put gas boilers out of business. perhaps the worst crisis is not the war in ukraine, or covid, or inflation, but the european commission itself. because after having brought the european automobile to its knees, attacked the mediterranean diet and italian excellence and opened the way for the collapse of the housing market, it now wants to take away from the citizens what it has for decades depicted as the clean source of heating, namely methane. i would say that we are facing yet another example of the genius of the euro-bureaucrats, who, as always, live in their bubble, detached from reality, a reality of ever-increasing bills and ever-increasing taxes and ever-increasing daily challenges to make ends meet. i dare not imagine the economic and employment fallout of this bold idea. i think it is time to get back on the ground and to structure policies that can be implemented within realistic time frames, without burying entire sectors and without all the costs falling on our citizens.

the next item is the report by mr jan huiteman, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and food safety, on the proposal for a directive on co2 emission standards for passenger cars and vans (com (2021) 0556 - c9-0322/2021 - 2021/0197 (cod)) (a9-0150/2022).
the rapporteur. the president, commissioner, and members of the european parliament. it has been a great honour for me to lead these negotiations on behalf of the european parliament. this regulation is the first of the 'fit for 55' package where the european parliament has reached an agreement with the member states and the european commission. the negotiations were concluded on 27 october 2022 and the council of ministers has in the meantime also approved the deal. the following issues were of great importance to the european parliament and i believe they are also clearly reflected in the outcome of the trialogue. the european parliament is committed to achieving the paris climate targets and the objectives of the european green deal. road transport still emits a significant amount of co2 and this amount is still increasing. the european parliament therefore maintains the objective of allowing only new co2-free cars on the european market by 2035. for example, consider electric cars powered by batteries and hydrogen cars. in addition to the climate, the european parliament attaches great importance to consumers and workers in the automotive sector. a lot of people need cars. the dependence on third countries makes fossil fuels more expensive and more taxable. in addition, consumers will have to pay a levy for the co2 emitted by their cars. a climate-friendly and affordable alternative is therefore of great importance. the cost of operating and maintaining an electric car, for example, is already lower than that of the combustion engine variant. with this legislation, the focus for investment and development will be on the further development of zero-emission cars. the forecast is therefore that the purchase price of these cars will fall and that a second- and third-hand market will be created more quickly. the european commission is required to produce an annual progress report which gives an insight into the transition in the automotive sector. where necessary, the eu will need to invest in, for example, retraining and training of workers, necessary charging infrastructure and last but not least the availability of critical raw materials needed for this transition. all in all, this new legislation will be a blow to the already established market situation. it will help consumers to continue to drive in an affordable way. it gives workers new opportunities and manufacturers investment security. it is an important climate measure and will reduce our dependence on fossil fuels from third countries.
executive vice-president of the commission. i would like to begin by paying a warm compliment to mr huitema and his shadow rapporteurs. they have negotiated a text on behalf of parliament which strikes a fantastic balance between the interests of industry, the interests of the climate and the interests of workers in particular. that's good work, and i compliment you on it. there's not much i can add to what jan just said. these are the arguments in favour of this legislation. i just want to put a little stress on a number of issues. first of all, as was already mentioned, we want people to have access to affordable mobility that is clean. and for that, you need to bring electric mobility, especially for passenger cars, up to scale as quickly as possible. so you have to be very, very clear about the choices you made. by the way, i think the european car industry has made that choice very clearly already, and they went they went a long way in the last three or four years in understanding where the global car industry is heading. yes, the running costs of electric vehicles are already lower than of combustion engine cars, and, within years, also buying an electric vehicle will be cheaper than buying a combustion-engine car. then you will help consumers make that choice much easier. so i think that's a very important argument in favour of this legislation. the second argument, which i want to pay a little attention to, because sometimes it's overlooked. look at what our competitors are doing globally. we are very proud of the fact that the european car industry is the best and the most innovative in the world. but we have to be very careful that we keep that advantage and that we keep producing cars that can match or even overtake what's produced elsewhere. just let me remind you that between last year and the end of this year china will bring 80 models of electric cars to the international market. these are good cars. these are cars that will be affordable more and more. and we need to compete with that. we don't want to give up this essential industry to outsiders. if we want to do that, we have to invest in transforming the european car industry, in making these evs electric vehicles for worldwide consumption. for that, we also need to enhance our efforts to build the charging infrastructure everywhere. this is something the commission is committed to, and i know this is important to the european parliament as well. finally, the most important part. we need to skill and reskill our workforce to have a sustainable job in the car industry of the future. we don't need fewer people; we need people with other skills. i need to insist on this because the industrial revolution is happening whether we like it or not. we can choose to lead it. we can choose to do it in a way that is socially compatible with our values. or we can leave it to other parts of the world to lead it, and then all we can do is follow and deindustrialize. we need to rebuild our industry on the basis of the future, and the car industry can lead this, if you vote for this proposal today.
the commission is also proposing to extend the scope of the directive to include the transport of dangerous goods. madam president, commissioner, colleagues, with a lot of work and intelligence, we have reached a historic agreement that reconciles the car and the climate. it's something, though, because normally they're two brothers who are enemies. it is a transitional agreement: it plans to end the production of thermal vehicles and replace them with zero-emission cars. we are at the very heart of great ecological theories, we are doing ecological planning. we have not made a great revolution, nor have we broken up when jobs are at stake, but we have switched off the last combustion engines, with a clever timetable and in concert with industry. we're shutting them down by 2035. the signal to industry is crystal clear: we must act now, because by 2035, it's over. i would remind you that transport accounts for a quarter of the european union's co2 emissions. light vehicles alone are responsible for about 10% of emissions in the eu. so, no choice: the automotive industry must play its part and do its part to achieve carbon neutrality by 2050. but if the automotive industry is playing along, this time we'll have to go along. let's create support for companies to move towards zero emission fleets. in particular, companies are the first buyers of new vehicles in europe, ahead of individuals. let us use the leasing tools in the company to promote electricity and hydrogen, as was done for petrol and diesel. let's create the financial incentive so that no electric vehicle is more expensive than its thermal equivalent. but, please, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, i see it too often: no more gigantism! no electric suvs for 40,000 euros! they have nothing ecological and they have nothing affordable. then we move on to the heavyweights: same effects on the climate, same mechanism. the european parliament will be able to look at it today, as the commission prepares to present its roadmap on co2 emissions from trucks. so, ladies and gentlemen, it is with great pride that i, as chairman of the committee on transport, am taking part in this march, in this measure, in the writing of a new page in history. i would therefore like to ask you to continue to show political courage in the coming months. this is the meaning of our fight for the climate, this is what the scientists ask us, this is our responsibility to the future generations, who are in the street, who are walking and asking us to act.

mr president, ladies and gentlemen, commissioner, and representative of the council, the ethical question is at the centre of a great deal of debate at the moment and deserves careful consideration. a political decision, if it is made with due regard for the interests and wishes of its representatives, is in every respect an ethical decision. unfortunately, we have recently seen how easily and lightly the ethical bond which underlies the democratic process between citizens and political representatives can be sacrificed for personal interests. in this context, full autonomy of the institutions in the control activity as well as in the monitoring of the conduct of parliamentarians and staff must be guaranteed, but a new interinstitutional body could be easily influenced or could carry out an unlawful coercive action. the criticisms raised during the debate in 2021 are still relevant, as one of the primary functions of the parliament, as expressly provided for in the treaty on european union, is to exercise political control, and consequently this role can never be delegated to an external authority. at the same time, there is no solid legal basis for the establishment of the body in question. the definition of competences for the body itself is therefore also rather approximate. in fact, to launch investigations on one's own initiative is in complete contradiction with the separation of powers and roles of the various institutions and entails situations of political interference. improving transparency and integrity is absolutely necessary, of course, but this must not be done by setting up a new and additional body, but by making existing institutions more accountable and empowering the authorities in charge.

structural effects of this inflation, but can only achieve the irreversibility of widespread deindustrialization, by adding to an unresolved supply-side problem also a demand crisis, related to
