i have three announcements to make before we enter the debate. on the one hand, following the resignation of mr nikos androulakis, parliament, acting on a proposal from the committee on legal affairs, shall, in accordance with the rules of procedure, declare its seat vacant with effect from 3 may 2023 and inform the competent national authorities.
the second news is that mr alexis georgoulis has left the left and is sitting with the non-attached members of our house, effective 19 april 2023.
the third message is a slightly longer one on the decisions of several committees on the opening of interinstitutional negotiations under article 71 (1) of the treaty, announced at the opening of the meeting on monday, 17 april. the president has received the request for a vote on the decision of the libe committee to open negotiations on the following procedures from the ecr and id groups and from non-attached members reaching at least the middle threshold, in accordance with rule 71 (2) of the rules of procedure: regulation on crisis management and force majeure; the screening regulation; regulation on asylum and migration management; regulation establishing a centralised system for identifying member states where information on convictions of third-country nationals and stateless persons is available; directive on long-term residents. the vote on all these items will take place tomorrow, thursday 20 april 2023. as regards the other decisions on the opening of interinstitutional negotiations, the president did not receive any request for a vote in parliament from members or from one or more political groups reaching at least the middle threshold. the committees can therefore start negotiations immediately.
the next item is the debate on the council and commission statements on 'children abducted from ukraine and the icc arrest warrant against vladimir putin' (2023/2640 ((rsp)).
president-in-office of the council. – mr president, honourable members, commissioner, when 12-year-old sasha was forcibly removed from his home in ukraine to a russian prison camp, he was separated from his mother. ‘they said that no one needs me, that i would get a new family, a russian family,’ he said. there are at least several hundred, perhaps thousands of ukrainian children like sasha who have become victims of a war crime. the council is firmly committed to ensuring full accountability for war crimes and the most serious crimes committed in connection with russia’s war of aggression against ukraine. as the european council underlined, russia must immediately ensure the safe return of ukrainians forcibly transferred or deported to russia, in particular children. in march, the european council took note of the arrest warrant issued by the international criminal court a few days earlier. these arrest warrants involved not only russia’s president vladimir putin, but also his commissioner for children’s rights, maria lvova-belova, for the alleged war crime of the unlawful deportation and transfer of ukrainian children from occupied territories of ukraine to russia. to investigate these war crimes the icc needs sufficient means and resources. therefore, over the last year, eu member states have provided additional support to the court as its workload has increased. at a conference for justice ministers in london in march, many member states indicated that they will continue to provide extra support in the near future. they also stated their intention to assist the icc in setting up a field office in kyiv. while the arrest warrants show how proceedings are progressing in the icc, i want to stress that the fight against impunity cannot solely rely on the international criminal court. for instance, the discussions about the establishment of a mechanism to prosecute the crime of aggression are well under way. the international centre for the prosecution of the crime of aggression in the hague, which will be supported by eurojust, is an important first step towards accountability for the crime of aggression. we hope to inaugurate the centre before the end of june. in its conclusions from december last year, the council insisted on the need to maintain our efforts on several other aspects related to the core international crimes committed in ukraine. this includes the support of national prosecutors and investigations both by ukrainian authorities and by the authorities in the member states. the joint investigation team and the new core international crimes evidence database are two concrete examples of these efforts. i would also like to point out that eurojust support for cooperation between the national level and the icc seems to be working well. the swedish presidency will, together with the commission, continue working to ensure a good coordination of all efforts to ensure accountability for the crimes committed in ukraine. thank you very much for your attention. i’m looking forward to the debate.
vice-president of the commission. – mr president, honourable members, thank you for organising this debate on a painful and urgent matter. on 17 march this year, the pre-trial chamber ii of the international criminal court issued warrants for the arrest of vladimir putin and maria lvova-belova, the russian commissioner for children’s rights for the war crime of unlawful forced deportation and transfer of ukrainian children to the russian federation. the fact that the court singled out the acts of unlawful forced deportation and transfer of ukrainian children as the war crimes, as a base for the first arrest warrants since the start of the russian invasion, illustrates both the urgency and severity rightly attached to the issue. using the pretext of evacuation, russia has started forcibly transferring and deporting ukrainian children from non-government controlled territories of ukraine to russia just before the launch of russia’s full-scale invasion in february 2022. the acts of deportation, or forcible transfer of children, have been reported by several international and ukrainian organisations, including the international commission of inquiry, the international committee of the red cross, the un human rights monitoring mission in ukraine, the un high commissioner for refugees bureau in russia and the national information bureau of ukraine. while the number of forcibly deported or transferred ukrainian children varies according to the source, the national information bureau of ukraine has received information of over 16 000 children concerned, as of last friday. the scale is, in other words, massive. the hague conference on private international law, of which russia is a member, has issued a statement recalling that international and national adoption procedures for children citizens of ukraine, shall not be carried out in any country of the world during the duration of martial law in ukraine, without due regard to the legislation of ukraine or pending the approval of the ukrainian authorities. unicef has issued a similar statement and ukraine has suspended its inter-country adoption programme until further notice in light of the russian war of aggression. adoption as a consequence of illegal deportation is a crime and shall not be tolerated. the un high commissioner for refugees’ office in russia and the independent russian tv channel dozhd have also identified ukrainian orphans in russian orphanages, some of which submit the children to the so-called military and patriotic education. the incidents identified by the international criminal court’s office of the prosecutor to base the arrest warrants on include the deportation of at least hundreds of children taken from orphanages and children’s care homes. in many cases, adoption of these children by russians has allegedly followed. the prosecutor alleges that these acts, amongst others, demonstrate an intention to permanently remove these children from their own country. at the time of these deportations, the ukrainian children were protected persons under the fourth geneva convention. the court also underlined that most acts in this pattern of deportations were carried out in the context of the acts of aggression committed by russian military forces against the sovereignty and territorial integrity of ukraine, which began already in 2014. as a result of the issuance of the arrest warrants, the international criminal court may transmit a request for the arrest and surrender of the suspects to any state of the territory of which those persons may be found and shall request the cooperation of that state in the arrest and surrender of the suspects. states parties to the court, including all eu member states, have an obligation to comply with requests for arrest and surrender. russia’s public reaction to these arrest warrants has further exemplified its blatant disrespect towards the international rules-based order. the eu has already publicly noted the arrest warrants against vladimir putin and maria lvova-belova, and welcomed and encouraged all further efforts to ensure full accountability for war crimes and the most serious crimes in connection with russia’s war of aggression against ukraine, towards the enforcement of international justice. we will continue our support to the international criminal court. since the start of this horrific war, the commission has provided over eur 10 million to the court to strengthen its investigation and prosecution capacities. we are also dedicated to support national investigations opened into international crimes committed in ukraine. in particular, the commission has extended the mandate of eurojust to collect and store evidence and strengthened it to be well equipped to support the joint investigation team uniting ukraine and six eu member states, in which the court’s office of the prosecutor participates as well. eurojust is competent to coordinate criminal investigations and prosecutions of individuals in cases where the deportation or transfer of population amounts to international crimes. following the request by the office of the prosecutor, competent national authorities participating in the joint investigation team have already shared information and evidence on deportations of children and can continue to do so in the future. this is crucial for case-building, such as regarding the cases against vladimir putin and maria lvova-belova. the chief prosecutor, karim khan, acknowledged that the participation of his office in the team, under the auspices of eurojust, has facilitated the swift coordination on relevant information and evidence. established in the framework of the joint investigation team and hosted by eurojust, the international centre for the prosecution of the crime of aggression against ukraine will provide a structure to coordinate investigations and exchange relevant evidence relating to the crime of aggression. with the prompt support of the european parliament, the regulation on eurojust has been amended, which allowed the agency to set up a database to store and analyse evidence of international crimes committed in ukraine. and we work closely with the office of the prosecutor general in ukraine, including to make sure they have all means at their disposal to conduct the investigations. honourable members, the eu also imposed sanctions against 16 individuals responsible for the deportation and forced adoption of ukrainian children from areas occupied by russian forces since 2022, including maria lvova-belova. the eu continues its efforts to identify perpetrators for possible further measures. looking ahead, we remain fully committed to make all efforts to ensure that those responsible for the crime of unlawful forced deportation or transfer of ukrainian children are held accountable and that the children of ukraine are returned safely to their families and communities. in march 2023, president von der leyen announced a joint european commission-poland initiative, in partnership with ukraine, aiming at bringing back these children. the starting point will be a conference to pull together international pressure to take all possible measures to establish the whereabouts of these children and bring them back home. according to the ukrainian children of war platform, only 361 children have so far returned to ukraine. in this light, the eu supports the mandate of the special representative of the un secretary-general on children and armed conflict to engage with ukraine and russia on the issue of deportation and children and child protection. we hope that information and data on ukrainian children and their status can be swiftly established and a returns mechanism instigated. as always, we will keep the well-being and best interests of the children at the forefront of all our actions. honourable members, thank you very much for your attention.
on behalf of the renew group. – mr president, madam commissioner, presidency, dear colleagues, war crime perpetrator putin is determined to destroy ukraine’s sovereignty, the lives of ukrainian people and the future of ukrainian children. the russian occupation authorities have forcibly and fraudulently deported or, to be more precise, kidnapped more than 20 000 ukrainian children. these innocent children are being illegally adopted on an accelerated basis and re-educated into russian citizens. the russian government, which is still a member of the united nations security council, unfortunately, is falsifying personal stories of ukrainian children, changing their names and erasing their identities. none of us would like to find ourselves in the shoes of the parents of these children. i call on the european union and the international community to use all possible means to locate, identify and return ukrainian children to their families, as well as to their homeland, ukraine.
on behalf of the ecr group. mr president, this debate was put on the agenda a few days before the world heard the words of some mercenaries from the wagner company, which i quote: "i carried out the order with this hand, i killed the children, including the five-year-old". these words probably didn't need to be added to the horror unleashed by vladimir putin, but they can help us understand the depth of what is happening in ukraine. there is no violence more despicable than that perpetrated against children. that is why we must shed full light on the allegations of the deportation of minors from the occupied areas of ukraine by the russian federation. we all know that the international arrest warrants issued against vladimir putin and belova are merely instruments of political pressure, nothing more. and unfortunately, we know that in the history of mankind, this has happened before and it's likely to happen again. what it has done and will always do is make the difference between being indifferent or not. let me end with a quote from edmund burke, the father of conservative thought: "for evil to triumph, it is enough for good men to do nothing".
mr president, thank you for your words. from the collective moral conscience of the rome statute of the international criminal court, which recalls the shared heritage that flows from the cultures of the peoples of the earth, they obtain the two arrest warrants for vladimir putin and maria lvova-belova for the alleged crimes of deportation of children from the occupied territories of ukraine into the russian federation. it is prohibited by international law for occupying powers to transfer civilians from the territory in which they live to other territories, and children enjoy special protection under the geneva convention, but their arrest will depend on international cooperation. if president putin goes to south africa for the brics summit next august, that country will be faced with the challenge of choosing between standing with the international community, respecting the common rules of coexistence among peoples, or protecting a man wanted for very serious crimes. the accession of south africa to the rome statute encourages us to strengthen all the instruments necessary to respect the rules-based international order. the european union must help save those children who are part of europe's future.
mr president, the accusation against russia of deporting ukrainian children was first raised by the ukrainian ombudsman lyudmila denisova, who was later fired by the kiev government for spreading too many lies. unfortunately, his accusations, which were never proven, were picked up by other ukrainian politicians and spread by various media. in reality, the so-called deportation concerns orphaned children who are taken in by russian families in safety, or children who are placed in the care of russians by their parents and brought to russia temporarily and then returned to their families. these children have become orphans because the ukrainian armed forces use the civilian population as human shields, as also reported by amnesty international. but russian president putin is held responsible for these alleged deportations and therefore hit by the arrest warrant of the international criminal court, never recognized by russia, but also by the united states and ukraine itself. it is not clear how this measure can achieve anything other than to further aggravate relations between europe and russia and to inhibit any possible attempt to find a negotiated solution to the conflict. let us stop with the instrumental and biased accusations, and work for peace if we want to save children.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, mr bettel, welcome to the european parliament. you appear before this house as the representative of a country, luxembourg, which has traditionally been at the forefront of european integration. more than seven decades after luxembourg laid the foundations of the benelux, our union is facing one of the greatest changes in our history. mr bettel, as you said in your speech, the time has come to decide how we want to continue to build europe. and i will add something to the elders: with whom do we want to continue to build europe? on behalf of the ecr group. madam president, mr prime minister, welcome to the house. the wonderful castles of luxembourg remind us of the history of our continent. a history of over a thousand years that has produced a civilization we are proud of, to love and preserve. i know that i am not saying anything new to you by reminding you that luxembourg is also famous for some overly aggressive tax practices. this allows many companies to reduce or avoid paying taxes in their home countries, creating unfair competition within the european union. i am sure you will agree with me that if we are to ensure proper economic development, we must defend the real economy against an economy based on finance alone. luxembourg is also home to schengen, where the agreement establishing a common area of free movement, which abolished the internal borders of the union, came into being. this is undoubtedly one of the most important achievements in cooperation between european states. the appalling paradox of today is that internal circulation is no longer as free as it should be, unlike external circulation. on the one hand, borders are being built to prevent secondary movements of illegal migrants between one european state and another, between france and italy for example. on the other hand, there is a call for the opening of the external borders to illegal immigration, turning the countries of first entry into huge refugee camps or open-air reception centres. this is often accompanied by public statements of severe criticism of the countries on the external border of the european union, because they are not sufficiently welcoming. but if an illegal immigrant manages to cross the internal border, he is caught like an animal by the police and deported to the state where he first landed. it is this hypocrisy that we have long denounced and that we are fighting against with firmness, realism and humanity. the europe we conservatives believe in has the breath of the great national stories that make it up. that is why we adopt the motto of luxembourg: "we want to remain what we are".
madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i believe that this is a very important moment for our future. it is clear that there are conflicting actions in the areas of industry and sometimes public interest, health, soil. i think we absolutely have to change the paradigm: we must no longer let money be the driver of our choices, but a vision that we must give to our children and future generations. so it is only by ensuring a european strategy based - as you have said, commissioner - on scientific evidence, coherence, synergies with the environment, labour, chemicals, water, soil and waste that we can ensure the full implementation of what the commission is proposing. the elimination of these perennial chemicals from the european production cycle is first and foremost a battle of civilizations. ensuring a clean environment while protecting public health is the challenge not of the next decade but of the next century.
in the name of the id group. madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, after a long and intense negotiation both in the econ committee and in the trilogue, we have arrived at the text of the regulation on markets in crypto-assets (mica), where a very great and excellent job has undoubtedly been done. i would like to point out, however, a few criticisms. the european union is the first to introduce rules in this area, but with the risk of catching up with the rapidly evolving world of crypto and crypto-assets, with the real danger of issuing an already outdated regulation. this is why i have always strongly supported the need to promote a world conference on crypto-assets, a kind of bretton woods, in order to plan and harmonize common rules between all actors, especially those outside europe. we have, however, achieved our objective of setting a few simple standards in a field which we could define as the wild west, and this is mainly to protect investors/consumers. however, we must not forget that we live in a highly globalised world of financial markets, where, in the absence of a common approach to the matter, enormous competition problems could arise due to the strong regulatory asymmetries. although it is widely believed that european regulations often add unnecessary red tape, in this case we can say that, by introducing the mica regulation, we have helped to give this sector a huge opportunity, by legally recognising it, and thus to have set a path for the future.
in writing. the pandemic and the ensuing crisis have highlighted the many problems facing health systems in individual member states. unfortunately, years of austerity-oriented budgetary policies have meant that over time, regular and intensive care beds and entire wards have been cut and there has been a reduction in all health care staff, doctors, nurses and other workers. today, with the worst of the pandemic behind us, it is necessary for the european union and its member states to learn from past mistakes by investing more in strengthening individual health systems. in this context, the eu, while respecting the national competences of individual states in health matters, must promote greater coordination by encouraging the exchange of good practice and the best technologies at our disposal. one area in which the eu must act and coordinate member states' activities is the fight against cancer: we need to quickly recover all the screening and diagnostics that were delayed due to covid-19, ensure that everyone has the best care, and invest more and more in medical and scientific research.
mr president, with the taliban in power, the status of women in afghanistan has gone backwards by 20 years. in the name of an archaic and extremist interpretation of sharia law, women have been forced to wear full veils and have been banned not only from working but also from studying. almost all girls' schools have been closed, and those who study or teach in secret are doing so at the risk of their lives. the recent arrest of matiullah wesa, who is guilty of promoting the education of girls and teenagers, shatters the last hopes of an entire generation of women who are being robbed not only of their rights but also of their future. if we are to be truly consistent in condemning the taliban regime, let us stress the need for the rights denied in afghanistan to be guaranteed in our civilized europe, where unfortunately many young immigrant women or daughters of immigrants are often initiated into early marriages instead of higher education with the shortsighted complicity of the left, which promotes the islamic veil instead of our western values of equality and freedom.
mr president, i must confess to being surprised at the choice of the title: "a call for further urgent measures". this is a report, the one we are discussing, which calls for radical changes in the food sector, in the energy sector, in transport, even in the food sector, in order to achieve the goal of reducing emissions. but the question is: what else do you expect us to do? while our global competitors are going in a completely different direction, here the illusion is fostered that tightening the belt to the point of suffocation will suffice to solve what is in fact a global problem and therefore requires a global solution. you have banned the sale of petrol cars, we have asked the member states to rebuild all their buildings, you have asked europeans not to eat meat, all in the name of the fight to cut emissions. and all this without any consideration for the social and economic repercussions that derive from this stigma of europe as the only one to blame for global warming. over 92% of emissions come from outside europe. this is a fact, and it means that the problem cannot be solved without the involvement of other countries, and if we in this house do not understand this, we are not going anywhere.
mr president, climate change is dividing the scientific community as well. some scientists believe that climate change is part of the natural cycle of world history, while others believe that it threatens the future of the planet. whatever the case, it is clear that our society has become more polluting since the industrial revolution. some say that to answer that question, mankind must "decline" in consumption and in lifestyles. they're the happy decline ones, but when has decline ever been happy in the history of the world? in the middle ages, nobody would have discussed co2 emissions, and environmentalists in 1200 would have all been unemployed. some say that the human population itself must decline, because humanity itself is not an opportunity, but a problem. they're the same ones, not by chance, who stain artwork and everything comes back: it is nihilism. i reject this approach which leads nowhere. the answer to the pollution of the planet is more development, not less. today, we're fortunate to have, for example, robots that collect plastic from the sea and clean up our rivers. we have the engineering of the scrubbers, which was unimaginable just 50 years ago. the technological and energy transition can happen, but it has to happen at the right time, otherwise we risk impoverishing people and starving them to death, not from overheating. we have the opportunity to live in balance with nature and to deal with climate change without succumbing, the solution is technology, progress, without hysteria and without catastrophism. if i had to choose between elon musk and greta thunberg, i'd choose musk. (the speaker agrees to reply to a 'blue card' speech)
the answer is "blue card". that's exactly what i told you. in the middle ages, there were no industries, there was no industrial revolution, but it is precisely thanks to the industrial revolution and, let's say, also thanks to emissions, that progress makes us live to 90 and not die at 20. unfortunately, it has contraindications, of course, the industrial revolution has pollution, but without that industrial revolution you would not be here today, we would not have come by car, we would not have returned home by plane. you want to go back to the middle ages, that's the problem. i agree that there must be an ecological transition, but we must do it at the right time, not by impoverishing people, not by degrowth. that's not the answer. the answer is progress.
madam president, commissioner, it is obviously necessary to review the role of de minimis, especially in the mountain areas of our continent. we know that doing business, doing industry, doing agriculture in mountain areas already entails inherent difficulties in the role that the territory plays for the people who live there, so it is necessary to take account of these peculiarities, as my colleagues before me have argued, whether for islands, remote areas or outermost areas, and to review this mechanism taking into account the peculiarities of the whole of european territory. europe is not a big flat box, all the same, but it is a large territory made up of different needs, different demands, different peculiarities. if we want to enable these territories to develop and achieve cohesion at a level acceptable at european level, we must take these difficulties into account in advance, otherwise we risk always leaving the usual behind and always favouring the others.

the minutes from yesterday’s sitting and the texts adopted are available. are there any comments? i see there are no comments, so the minutes are approved.
the next item on the agenda is the commission statement on the eu action plan against trafficking in cultural goods (2023/2646(rsp)).
member of the commission. – mr president, dear members, the art and antiquities market was valued at almost usd 68 billion globally in 2022, and demand keeps on growing. the market for cultural goods has some vulnerabilities attractive to criminals: buyers can be anonymous, the price of art is subjective and items can be moved easily. the illegal status of a cultural object is not immediately visible, like with weapons or drugs. this helps traffickers too. for all this, the illicit trafficking in cultural goods is appealing for organised criminal groups. they operate across borders using shell companies, free ports and cash to hide the money trail. they also benefit from the increasing online sales where provenance and authenticity checks are less thorough. interpol counted over 850 000 artefacts seized in 2020, more than half in europe, and this may only be the tip of the iceberg. now, what can we do against that? we are not powerless against this situation and we are already doing quite a lot. many eu countries have law enforcement units that specialise in cultural property crimes. the amount of goods seized in europe proves their positive impact. the commission supports europol’s work to tackle this crime within the european multidisciplinary law enforcement platform called empact. as an example, pandora is an annual law enforcement operation involving many member states, europol, interpol, the us and others. so far, operation pandora has led to more than 400 arrests, resulting in the recovery of almost 150 000 cultural goods since 2016. now about the action plan. to further address these challenges, the commission presented the eu action plan against trafficking in cultural goods last december. this was announced in the 2021 eu strategy to tackle organised crime. it is a comprehensive plan to fight organised crime effectively and to protect cultural heritage within and beyond the eu. to succeed with this task, the action plan has four strategic objectives. one: prevention and detection of crimes by market participants and cultural heritage institutions. two: leveraging law enforcement and judicial capabilities. three: international cooperation, including source and transit countries. and four: support from all key stakeholders to protect cultural goods from crime. the action plan sets out what the commission will do and what member states are encouraged to do. on the commission side, we will help improving the intelligence picture to make cultural goods trafficking more visible. we will consider measures to improve the traceability of cultural goods transactions across member states, and we will close loopholes on the illicit trade of cultural goods to export and import regulation and border controls. we also will enhance our dialogue with the art market. importantly, the commission already supports source and transit countries. we are supporting ukraine, for instance, to protect and evacuate its cultural heritage endangered by the russian war of aggression, and we are working with unesco on fighting illicit trafficking of cultural goods in the western balkans. for member states, the action plan contains several recommendations. to take more advantage of europol’s and eurojust’s support to reinforce law enforcement and judicial cooperation in this field. to sign, ratify and implement the council of europe nicosia convention on offences relating to cultural property. to boost the capacity of national law enforcement and judicial authorities. they can do it by setting up and training specialised law enforcement units and prosecutors, or providing basic training for police and customs. finally, member states should ensure that public and private collections register their possessions and report property crimes. it is clear that we all have a role to play when it comes to stepping up the fight against trafficking of cultural goods, whether it is at eu level or national or just stakeholder level.
on behalf of the s&d group. mr president, commissioner schmit, in 2020 over 850 000 cultural goods, including paintings, sculptures, archaeological objects and library materials, were seized globally. more than half of these items were seized in europe. this data conceals a problem of vast proportions, since many cases are still not detected. cultural heritage is of enormous artistic, historical and scientific importance. illicit trafficking can have a devastating and irreversible impact on cultural heritage, destroying parts of our collective memory and depriving citizens of testimonies of their history. among the actions proposed in the action plan, i believe that the promotion and strengthening of international cooperation is essential, especially with third countries, since this is a crime of a cross-border nature. european action cannot be confined to the union's borders, but must go beyond them. the commission should therefore respond to requests from third countries as well. such as the establishment of a policy dialogue at ministerial level to promote the protection of cultural heritage from illegal activities. countries such as italy have been recognised as examples to follow in this area, and italy was the first country in the world to set up and make available to unesco the 'blue helmets of culture' task force. similarly, many latin american countries are progressively strengthening their policies to protect against the sale of archaeological finds. a high-level political dialogue would allow for fruitful exchange with the aim of further facilitating and strengthening international cooperation. there are still weaknesses in data collection. some states not only lack specialized units, but also lack national databases dedicated to the registration of stolen artworks and linked to those of interpol. the lack of these elements also limits the receipt of information on an international scale. finally, data collection must be accompanied by a strong awareness-raising activity in schools, among local communities, in universities, as there is still little awareness of the damage that illicit trafficking in cultural goods can cause. the illegal traffic is therefore a serious, heinous crime, because it takes away the common goods from the public enjoyment, transforming the use value, the beauty available to all into exchange value and the commodity available to the very few. it is our responsibility to do our utmost to ensure the recovery of a heritage that belongs to all humanity.
for the greens/efa group mr president, ladies and gentlemen, for the last 25 years, under the guise of economic rationality, austerity has been set up as a dogma in europe. a dogma with its apostles who pose as guardians of good management, these fetishes that take the form of deficit and debt ratios that have no scientific basis, and of course its morality that exalts the sense of collective effort and sacrifice. collective effort and sacrifice, you say? but for whose benefit? as is often the case, dogma is not neutral. it serves the interests of a few at the expense of the many. the reality is that austerity has always been applied selectively in europe. on the one hand, the priests of the fiscal orthodoxy wanted to systematically cut public investment in education and health, and sell public assets to the private sector. on the other hand, from the financial crisis to the war in ukraine to the pandemic, it's a bunch of crazy. i mean a package of public money that the same people pour into the big business without conditions and without bargaining. this is evidenced by the huge profits in sectors such as fossil fuels and finance. this selective austerity is creating a double injustice, both social and climate. it increases the level of public debt to ultimately enrich the rentiers of the system without benefiting the greatest number of our fellow citizens or the planet. the real issue is not whether to choose between the supposedly frugal and the spendthrift, between the supposedly virtuous and the promiscuous. no, i'm not going. the only question worth asking is: what is our public debt to be used for? should it finance fossil fuels, bank bailouts, tax breaks for the richest, or, on the contrary, fight climate change, make our societies more just and resilient in the uncertain world that is ours? this is the second choice we make in our green party. that is why we want a thorough review of the budgetary rules. firstly, we call for the deactivation of the deficit rule, which has no scientific value. what counts is not so much the deficit figure as the public, including economic, utility of the expenditure it finances. secondly, we propose that the sustainability of debt should now be assessed in the light of climate risk. the reality is that the best way to make our public finances unsustainable is to delay investing in the green transition. on the contrary, we have every interest in speeding them up, and our rules must allow this. these reforms are aimed at moving towards an economic strategy that is no longer governed by figures but by needs. in short, more public debt today for less ecological, social and financial debt tomorrow. that is the political project we are advocating.
president. - the next item is the report (a4-0179/97) by mr pimenta, on behalf of the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy, on the proposal from the commission to the council (com (97) 0173 - c4-0179/97). the government debt must not exceed, or be allowed to exceed, 60% of gdp and the budget deficit must be limited to 3%. thirty years after the adoption of these principles, we have to note that the average debt level in the eu is 85% and the average budget deficit is 3.6%. in the eurozone, these figures are even slightly higher. and in its latest forecast, the imf actually sees little improvement, even to 2028. the commission has never sanctioned a member state which has not complied with the rules, especially not in the case of larger member states. the financial markets did so by charging higher interest rates. the commission proposes new budgetary rules with a debt reduction programme tailored to the customer: each member state shall negotiate its debt restructuring. any reform or adjustment will ultimately stand or fall with the proverbial stick behind the door and its application. and in both cases the new commission proposals do not really provide for anything drastic. only in this way can greedy governments be kept in line. the budget deficits and debts must be reduced, if we are to win the fight against inflation, prevent further financial instability and ultimately preserve monetary union.
in the name of the id group. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, it is certainly a positive aspect that a proposal has finally been put forward to revise rules which, as everyone was aware ten years ago, were wrong rules for the management of public finances and investment within the european union. however, the commission proposal is based, in my view, on three erroneous assumptions which need to be corrected. the first is that it does not change the incorrect implementation of the rules of fiscal coordination within the european union, assuming that by now all the institutions, from the international monetary fund to the european central bank and the leading economists, have recognized that the great debt crisis of the eurozone was not a public debt crisis, but was a crisis of an imbalance of private debt, especially foreign debt. and in proof of this, we know that some of the countries that ended up on the programme, i am thinking of portugal, i am thinking of spain, i am thinking of ireland, had a public debt-to-gdp ratio in line with or even below the 60% provided for in the treaties. the second point is that it does not change the setting of these figures, the 60% and the 3%, which are figures that have no economic basis but are not reviewed. the third point, in my view, is a matter of democratic accountability: it is right to create a flexible path for each member state but the proposal gives too much discretion to the european commission, which is not an elected body and which, unfortunately, runs the risk of being able to use this power to shape the policies of a government and we know that these plans very often could go beyond the duration of a national government and therefore affect policies. the last point concerns investment. if we look at the data, unfortunately many countries in the european union will be forced into fiscal consolidation which will not allow us to make the investments we need for industrial transition.
mr president life has really overruled our rules of economic governance. at the same time, these new proposals must be tested to see whether they strike a balance between growth and responsible management, whether they simplify everything and whether they measure all member states equally. i have to say, i'm not comfortable with that. firstly, i believe in the restoration of budgetary balance, not in the indebtedness of countries and the union. by abolishing the debt ceiling, the commission will be able to judge individual member states on uncertain grounds. this undermines predictability and jeopardises the principle of equal treatment. secondly, it is not clear on what grounds the commission may propose to open an excessive deficit procedure, and there is a need for safeguards to ensure that the commission does not abuse its discretion. third, medium-term fiscal structural plans are good in terms of predictability, but in the current challenging environment, fiscal policy should have the flexibility to act as a buffer if needed in the event of disruption in other sectors of the economy. fourth, the overloading of the european semester with new areas leads to debate and a loss of focus. finally, the powers of the independent fiscal institutions are very different. i do not think that regulation at eu level is important. these five dilemmas need to be addressed, because only then will there be a balance between credible, predictable debt reduction and the fiscal flexibility needed to address the challenges.
mr president, dear colleagues, the debate that we are having today is a very fundamental debate for the european union. it is not a technical debate. debates about fiscal rules and about public budgets in europe are debates about and decisions about people’s money. our obligation is to manage people’s money with great care, to make sure that the next generations have more opportunities than the previous generations. this is why we need to get the objective of this fundamental debate right. the objective is not to make more depth in europe. the objective is to allow our economies to grow, to generate jobs and to generate wealth while we keep debt low in europe. because we have all seen that too high levels of debt are a risk. they are a vulnerability for our economies, particularly in times of crisis. this is why we as epp group say very clearly our economies need to grow faster than our debt grows, and in order to allow our economies to grow, reforms are important, competitiveness is important. and yes, while we recognise that different countries are in different situations and they have different paths towards debt sustainability, the overall objective needs to be for a debt to be low because only with low debt we can help in times of crisis. we could at the beginning of covid create the largest package of economic support because still we had space of manoeuvre in europe and we need to we need to manage this with great care. we as epp group ask for clear rules, predictability, no exemptions, agreements between the commission and member states and these be respected.
mr president, a good economic governance must have two characteristics – ambition and credibility – and credibility does not rhyme necessarily with the severity as many claim. credibility is measured by the economic, social and political sustainability of those rules. and if rules are not sustainable over time, governance is not credible. so the old governance was perhaps ambitious, but not particularly credible – it hasn’t proved that over time. the review launched by the commission shall set the right balance between ambition and credibility. the proposals are already moving in the right direction, but frankly, i believe that something more needs to be done to guarantee an adequate level of public investment, because these investments are key to growth in private investments as well, and these will ensure the digital, environmental and social transition challenges that we have ahead of us and this is what really we need to do to meet our citizens’ expectations. so, more investments today means more growth, more jobs for tomorrow and in the end, that’s what we are called to deliver. this is what citizens expect from us. this is how we can be credible.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i can only give a brief reflection on the reform of the stability and growth pact. in the reform the commission will have enormous powers in determining the economic policies of national governments, much more than in the previous pact, not even involving this parliament which, i remind you, is democratically elected by universal suffrage, as are the parliaments of each of the 27 countries of the union. it follows that in governments which are acceptable to the commission the new rules will be interpreted, while in others they will be applied rigidly. it will no longer be the citizens who decide by voting, but the unelected bureaucrats who have always shown themselves to be completely disconnected from reality, the same ones who made the incomprehensible decision not to take nextgenerationeu loans out of the calculation of the public debt. i firmly believe that sovereignty belongs to the people and that those who think of replacing them will fail miserably. then i heard earlier the statement that the euro has become like the lira; i would remind you that in 1991, italy was the fourth industrial power in the world in terms of the lira. the problem is that you have to be able to do things, not with a central bank that can't.
mr president, ladies and gentlemen, mr vice-president, i have a question for you today. what are the macroeconomic science bases for the 60% of gdp debt to 3% of gdp deficit parameters which are still not being removed or changed? because, you see, i've been looking for this answer for years, and i can't find it, because the study by the two oxford professors who supported the 60% debt-to-gdp ceiling turned out to be completely wrong. for years, there has been talk of reforming the stability pact and there was an expectation that action would be taken on its main problem, namely the thresholds, especially after the pact had to be suspended in the covid-19 pandemic precisely because they were not sustainable thresholds. instead, we are faced with a system of unfounded and reinforced constraints that will force italy to cut between 8 and 15 billion euros a year in spending and to make reforms and investments decided in brussels, which leave out works crucial to the country's recovery and will erode current spending on welfare and maintenance of essential infrastructure. this is not the recipe for recovery, nor for the social stability of a europe which does not want to leave anyone behind.
madam president, mr chancellor scholz, as the five star movement we have a very clear idea of the europe of the future: greener, more united and above all free from war. on these issues your country is doing its homework, let us think of the minimum wage and the billion euro plan for renewables, but in brussels it has a very different attitude which prevents all other states from making the same progress as germany. i am thinking of reforming the stability pact. the position of your government, expressed repeatedly by your minister of finance, is a dive into the past that takes us back to the dark years of austerity, when decimals of gdp were more important than people's lives. without new resources, member states could never meet the european objectives of the new green deal and the sustainable transition. green investments should therefore be excluded from the new calculation of the pact. finally, a thought. we are all here today to talk about the future of europe, but there can be no future without a peaceful present. european diplomatic action is urgently needed to end the war in ukraine. peace is built through dialogue and not through arms, which, moreover, should not be financed by european funds.
madam president, ladies and gentlemen, the dramatic problem of the increase in violence against women must be a priority. while many groups talk about gender equality and fighting inequalities by putting forward vague proposals, we should all focus on concrete solutions that need to be given by the member states. the istanbul convention, which is intended to introduce gender ideology, is not a solution. what we need is something else, not lgbt ideology. i honestly don't think that a woman who's been raped, a girl who's been bullied or a girl who's been bullied online would be particularly interested in having non-stereotypical gender roles taught in schools. what is really needed is to find, for example, a ready response from the authorities: we know that many times there is no complaint because of the slowness of the responses and the concern about the repercussions between the moment of the fact and the adoption of the measures. i've heard too many times the phrase "okay, you tell me to report, but before decisions are made what do i do? do i have to barricade myself inside?" that is why i am proud of the government of my party, because in italy it has been the promoter of the red code and, a few days ago, of the so-called 'enhanced' code. that's what it takes, because we have to say it clearly: the acceptable number of women who are victims of violence, of any kind, must be zero.
